{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgF1b3eqWErkv6tEhg8Cv","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've written `/app/answer.py` and run it, which produced `/app/answer.json` with all 24 farms. The final script's totals match the ones I got during exploration.\n\n| Farm | kWh | Farm | kWh | Farm | kWh |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| A | 3936 | I | 8701 | Q | 14876 |\n| B | 10869 | J | 6125 | R | 7832 |\n| C | 3718 | K | 9593 | S | 10355 |\n| D | 7388 | L | 7933 | T | 11345 |\n| E | 8251 | M | 8935 | U | 6223 |\n| F | 3308 | N | 5440 | V | 9243 |\n| G | 8264 | O | 11099 | W | 5982 |\n| H | 5276 | P | 3112 | X | 7949 |\n\n**How the data was handled:**\n- **Which farms:** farm names in `farms.csv` have stray spaces and tabs; I cleaned them and matched them to `farm_registry.csv`. That gives 24 farms, so I left out the 10 extra files (FARM_Y to FARM_AH).\n- **Reading the files:** the files mix UTF-16 and UTF-8, comma and semicolon separators, and European number formats like `18.000,0000`. Each file starts with three report lines before the column header, which I skipped.\n- **Which value column:** several files have two columns with the same name, one with a trailing space. I used the exact name from the calibration file, spaces included. That column's overnight reading always equals the calibration offset, which confirms it; the other copy is a decoy.\n- **Timestamps:** both `YYYY-MM-DD` and `DD.MM.YYYY` parse. Invalid ones like `2024-13-40 88:99:00` and blanks are dropped. The timestamps are in each farm's local time (production always runs about 06:00–18:00 local), so I converted them to UTC before applying the 2024-03-15 window.\n- **Duplicates:** every `888888.8` spike is followed by a re-sent row with the same timestamp and the real value. `drop_duplicates(subset=timestamp, keep='last')` removes all of them.\n- **Calibration:** I subtracted the offset first, then dropped negatives. This keeps FARM_B readings like `-0.0144` MWh, which are real once calibrated.\n- **Units:** converted Wh, MWh and BTU to kWh. For power sensors I multiplied kW by the reading interval (5, 15 or 30 minutes).\n- **Maintenance:** I excluded only the two completed windows: Vistula on 15 Mar 12:00–14:00 (8 readings) and Sakura on 16 Mar 08:00–12:00 local, which overlaps the end of the UTC day (4 readings). The cancelled Sakura inspection is not excluded.\n- **Safety rule:** the 45-minute shutdown rule is in the code but never triggers, because no inverter temperature goes above 85 °C (the highest is about 74 °C).\n\n**Judgment calls that could differ from the expected answer:**\n- **`-999.0` as an error code:** I dropped these readings. Otherwise, at the four farms with large negative offsets, calibration would turn them into positive energy. Keeping them would raise FARM_E by 72 kWh and FARM_N, S and W by 3 kWh each.\n- **Maintenance end time:** I excluded readings from the start time up to but not including the end time, the same way as the reporting day.\n- **Readings above capacity:** I didn't cap them.
